Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Tyler Sullivan24 August, 2017 at 21:28
I love how the release from Panini says it’s a “new product built from the ground up”, yet it’s essentially just mash-up of Elite and Unparalleled. Plus it’s sad that we’re almost to week 1 of the NFL season and have not had a single Myles Garrett auto or jersey card yet. I realize he’s a DE for the Browns, but there is still a market for the guy. Just seems strange to me he’s been M.I.A. in all brands.
Ryan Cracknell28 August, 2017 at 11:26

Sean Rivera25 March, 2017 at 18:22
I’ve moved to Score after Topps stopped doing football. I like that you continue to offer a product that new collectors and those on a budget can enjoy.
Ron Geiger13 April, 2017 at 18:35
The best thing about Score Football is it is affordable to everyone. Panini is making to many high price sets with very few cards per box. If they keep it low priced and lots of cards it may bring back the hey day of cards. At the rate they are going it will price the collecting out of reach.I use to collect all of Topps, Score, Donruss, & Upper Deck.Now I only buy about 3 different sets.I cannot afford any More. Thats what makes Score so nice and lots of fun to open. Make more low price set, lets get thee kids involed again.
Thanks R
Tommy Smith21 April, 2017 at 22:32
I have been an avid Football collector since 1974. I absolutely love football cards. Since the hobby has made it an exclusive for just one company , it has hurt the collector in my opinion. I love the 1989 Score set way more than the current one. Honestly I would love to see a Pro Set type set come back. Collectors in my opinion have been forgotten over time.
Steve8 November, 2017 at 12:49
